Changelog

All notable changes to Eshkol will be documented in this file.

The format is based on Keep a Changelog, and this project adheres to Semantic Versioning.

[Unreleased]

[1.3.5-evolve] - 2026-09-22

Release date: Tuesday, September 22, 2026. The callee-identity, type-precision and ICC-invariant hardening changes are integrated. The entries below record the source changes; the verification record for the tagged commit is the “Final verification” section of RELEASE_NOTES.md.

  • swish on the VM. (swish x [beta]) was a native-only builtin; the VM now defines it with the native semantics (x * sigmoid(beta * x), a number or a tensor like x, differentiable through the number case).

  • make-string checks its arguments and repeats any character. Natively (make-string "xyz") read the string’s address as a length and faulted, and a non-ASCII fill was cut to one byte ((make-string 2 #\λ) had length 0); the VM answered "" for a non-integer or negative length, filled a non-ASCII or non-character fill with spaces, and silently capped the length at 65536. Both engines now raise a catchable type error unless the length is an exact non-negative integer and the fill a character, and repeat the fill’s UTF-8 encoding.

  • The standard ports are parameter objects on the VM. On the bytecode VM current-output-port was an undefined variable, so (parameterize ((current-output-port p)) ...) failed, and current-input-port did not exist either (SW-250). The three standard ports are now parameter objects on the VM as natively, and every read or write that names no port follows them. Natively, read with no port now reads the current input port, as read-char and read-line already did.

  • Record predicates tell record types apart on the VM, and accessors check their operand on both engines. The VM’s record predicate was vector?, so a plain vector passed it and cat? of a dog was #t; its tag was a string and it indexed fields in declaration order, not constructor order (SW-249). The VM now compiles the native lowering: a type-symbol tag, a predicate that checks it, and fields in constructor order. On both engines an accessor or mutator applied to a value that fails the predicate raises "<accessor>: not a <type> record" instead of reading the vector.

  • VM hash tables find keys by equal? and return what was stored. The bytecode VM’s table keyed on a Value’s raw payload and returned every value as an integer, so (hash-ref h "origin" #f) missed a key spelled by another string, a stored record came back as its heap slot number (an accessor on it then raised), and hash-clear! returned an empty copy instead of clearing (SW-247). Keys and values are now boxed Values hashed and compared by equal?, as on the native backend.

  • Builtins used as values accept what their call form accepts. + - * / as values were 2-argument closures, so a list apply through a variable ((define g +) (apply g '(1 2 3)), and partial, for-each-style helpers built on it) raised “expected fixed-arity procedure”; they are now variadic closures over the reduction apply itself uses. That reduction answered 5 for (apply - '(5)) and 2 for (apply / '(2)); a single argument is now the inverse and - or / of no arguments is an error. display, write and newline as values had arity 0 and ignored a port; they now take their optional port on the native backend and the VM, and every output and input builtin raises a catchable type error for an argument in the port position that is not a port of the right direction, where (newline 1) used to fault. (procedure-arity +) is 0, the fixed count of a variadic procedure. gradient of a variadic callable reached at run time spreads every coordinate of the point across its fixed parameters and rest list; a (a . rest) loss used to receive the whole point as one vector.

  • Renamed binders keep their source spelling, and a local binding shadows every builtin. The hygienic expander renames every lexical binder, and the implementation name leaked: type diagnostics read argument 2 of '_v972.loop' and linear variable '_v973.b', and (display (lambda (x) x)) printed (lambda (_v975.x) _v975.x). Diagnostics and procedure source forms now name identifiers as written. A local walk, unify, make-fact or other builtin the parser lowers to its own operation was ignored inside its scope once renamed, so the builtin ran instead; the expander now turns those uses into calls of the local binding.

  • A top-level begin did not splice its definitions (SW-244). Natively (begin (define tt 5) 1) followed by tt failed with Undefined variable: the parser rewrote every begin holding a definition into a scoped letrec. At the top level a begin now splices its forms into the program (R7RS 5.1), through nested begins, a begin in a top-level with-region body, and macros that expand to a begin of definitions, on the native compiler and the bytecode VM.

  • Region promotion is all-or-nothing. When a store’s promotion out of a region could not allocate its copy, the barrier logged, returned the unpromoted pointer and the store published it; a parent copied before a failed child left an older-arena object pointing into the region about to be freed (SW-232, #713). A promotion is now one transaction: on failure nothing is stored, the region and destination arena are unchanged, and a catchable region promotion: out of memory error object is raised. Bulk vector and tensor stores promote before they store. A parameter binding (make-parameter, parameterize) is promoted before the binding is published, so a failed promotion leaves the previous binding in force. A failpoint matrix fails every allocation a promotion depends on, at every occurrence, and checks that each failure leaves nothing behind. See ADR-0001’s 2026-09-22 amendment.

  • Malformed tensor shapes raise on every engine (#550). (reshape v 1.5 2) no longer fails to compile natively, the VM accepts the documented variadic (zeros d1 d2 ...) and (ones d1 d2 ...) and validates their shape, and indexing an empty tensor is refused before any arithmetic. The malformed_shape_matrix test runs the issue’s reproducers and the related shape rules on the JIT, AOT, the VM and the browser VM.

  • CUDA architectures follow the installed toolkit (#606). The portable default list is resolved against nvcc --list-gpu-arch (or the toolkit version’s documented range when nvcc cannot answer), so CUDA 13 configures without SM72 and CUDA 12 keeps it. An explicit CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES is used as given.

  • A caught condition printed an error anyway. Natively the runtime wrote ERROR: Type error in vector-ref: ... (and similar) to stderr before unwinding to the guard that caught it. A condition is now reported only when no handler is installed; a caught one prints nothing, on native JIT, AOT and the VM. string->symbol of a non-string raises a catchable type error on both engines (natively it faulted; the VM answered #f).

  • A top-level with-region or begin lost the definitions in its body on the VM (SW-240), and a with-region inside a procedure lost its internal definitions (SW-239): the name then read an unrelated value, for example (begin (define tt 5) 1) left tt as 1. Both now bind as natively.

  • Number syntax is one grammar, complex numbers included (SW-223). The source parser split 1+1i into 1 and +1i, the bytecode VM read it as a call, (string->number "1+1i") answered #f while read returned a symbol, #i42 was exact and #e1.5 was not a number. Every reader – program literals, read and string->number, on the native compiler and the VM – now asks one recognizer for the full R7RS grammar: a+bi, a-bi, +i, -i, +bi, polar m@a, #e/#i and #b/#o/#d/#x prefixes, rational and infinite or NaN parts (ADR-0028). 1+0i is the exact integer

    1. #e on a non-real complex number, an infinity or a zero denominator is a compile error in a program, a read error for read, and #f from string->number. string->number accepts the R7RS radices 2, 8, 10 and 16 (it had accepted up to 36). A native read of an integer past int64 had clamped to INT64_MAX; it is now the exact bignum. A symbol spelled like a number is written with bars (|+i|).

  • A container accessor read the wrong kind of container as its own (SW-221). (vector-ref (list 1 2 3) 0) answered 8 natively and () on the bytecode VM; (vector-length (list 1 2 3)) answered 4097 and 0; string-ref of a list answered a control character natively and NUL on the VM; (string-ref 5 0) and (tensor-ref (list 1 2) 0) faulted natively. The vector, string, bytevector and tensor-ref accessors now pass their operand through one container check per engine (TaggedValueCodegen::requireContainer natively, vm_require_container in the VM) and raise a catchable error for any other kind. The VM’s inline string opcodes and its first-class string natives now share one implementation; the threaded copy indexed bytes, so (string-ref "héllo" 1) answered a byte of é rather than the character.

  • A condition the native runtime raised on its own was not an error object. car of a non-pair, an arithmetic type error, bignum and rational division by zero, i128 overflow and a forward-referenced stub were built by a header-less exception constructor, while raise hands every exception to a handler as a heap object classified by its header. A guard clause therefore saw garbage: (guard (e (#t (error-object? e))) (car 5)) answered #f and e displayed as a list of addresses, where the bytecode VM answered #t. Every exception object now carries its header, built in one place.

  • apply spreading too many elements into a fixed-arity procedure raised a type error. The refusal said Type error in apply: expected fixed-arity procedure, got procedure. It is now an arity error rendered by the shared formatter, Arity mismatch: <procedure> expects 1 argument but got 3. Runtime error messages raised with no recorded source location also no longer begin with stray bytes from an uninitialized location-prefix buffer.

  • Every unary numeric builtin keeps a Taylor derivative. asin, acos, atan, asinh, acosh, atanh, log2, log10, exp2, cbrt, atan2 and the rounding functions returned their primal on a Taylor tower or level, so (derivative-n atan 1.0 2) was 0 and a first-order derivative nested in a derivative-n of atan was 0 (SW-210). They are now one recurrence, s_k = (1/k) Σ j u_j d_{k-j} with d = f'(u), in every carrier tier; exact inputs keep every rational coefficient ((derivative-n atan 0 3) is -2). The code generator reads its tower routes from taylor_recurrences.def, and taylor_unary_route_guard fails when a builtin has no route.

  • Complex expt, log, angle and the inverse functions keep the derivative a complex value carries. (derivative (lambda (w) (expt w 3)) 1+1i) was 0 and (derivative-n log 1+1i 1) lost its imaginary part (SW-211). Every procedure with a plain complex kernel now has a carrier formula.

  • Every math builtin is a first-class value. atan2 passed, stored or returned was the raw C function (a crash) and (apply atan2 ...) did not compile; a first-class atan or round dropped its second argument. The math builtins now take one table-driven value route, with atan and round dispatching on their argument count (SW-230).

  • A raise or an escape out of a derivative leaves no AD state behind. The forward pass level stayed raised, so later derivatives lost exactness and (derivative (lambda (b) (/ 1 b)) 0) answered -inf.0 instead of raising after an earlier raise (SW-229).

  • Powers at a zero base have the closed form’s derivatives. On a Taylor tower sqrt, expt with a constant exponent and the inverse functions’ derivative series answered NaN at 0.0, because the power recurrence divides by the base. (derivative-n sqrt 0.0 2) is now -inf.0, (derivative-n (lambda (x) (expt x 2.0)) 0.0 2) is 2, and |x| written as sqrt(x*x) stays NaN (SW-225).

  • A derivative through a simple pole is the closed form’s infinity on every engine. (derivative (lambda (x) (/ 1.0 x)) 0.0) answered +nan.0 natively and -inf.0 on the VM; it is -inf.0 everywhere, the second derivative +inf.0, and (derivative-n cbrt 0 1) is +inf.0. Jet division runs the series division recurrence, and a zero perturbation coefficient no longer turns an infinity into NaN; an indeterminate x * (1/x) or a double pole stays NaN (SW-222).

  • square, inexact and magnitude of a real carrier keep the derivative. They answered 0 on a jet or a tower, and square of a rational or a complex number printed pointer bits (SW-214). ### Added

  • WebGPU GPU dispatch in the browser. The ordinary GPU dispatch that selects Metal or CUDA now selects WebGPU in the browser: eshkol_matmul_dispatch and the eshkol_gpu_* entry points are served by web/eshkol-webgpu.js (mirrored to site/static/), which both WASM loaders install, and by gpu_memory_webgpu.cpp in Emscripten builds. The f64 kernels are sf64 – IEEE binary64 on integer words, like Metal’s metal_softfloat.h – so the default exact tier gives matmul and elementwise results bit-identical to the CPU path, and reductions within 1e-9. Without WebGPU, JSPI or an adapter, initWebGPU() reports why and the program runs on the CPU; with a device, every call the GPU does not serve is counted and explained in the backend’s diagnostics. scripts/lib/webgpu_diff_runner.mjs gates every kernel against the CPU in Chrome, including random f64 bit patterns and edge values, and proves itself red on five kernel corruptions. See docs/breakdown/GPU_ACCELERATION.md, “Enabling WebGPU in a page”.

  • WebGPU for the browser bytecode VM. The VM’s tensor natives already call the ordinary GPU seam; the WASM VM build now links it (gpu_memory_webgpu.cpp), and EshkolWebGPU.attachVm() connects it to the page’s WebGPU backend through JSPI. Its compute imports become suspending, and repl_eval/run_program become promising, only when the browser has JSPI and a device (ADR-0029). The VM’s matmul, same-shape elementwise arithmetic and full reductions run on the same sf64 kernels as compiled programs. Without WebGPU or JSPI the VM runs on the CPU and reports why. The site’s REPL and runnable examples use it. The VM builds with native wasm exceptions, because JSPI cannot suspend across Emscripten’s JavaScript setjmp/longjmp trampolines. tests/webgpu/webgpu_vm_test.mjs gates it in Chrome.

Fixed

  • Navier-Stokes evidence scope (SW-251). Public documentation, the ICC oracle, and release text now describe residuals, similarity profiles, scaling identities, and energy estimates. The renamed evidence ledger and checker retain all 84 computational rows and their existing status counts.

  • The bytecode VM now grows its call-frame array for non-tail recursion, bounded by ESHKOL_VM_MAX_FRAMES_CEILING, instead of failing at the initial 256-frame allocation. Continuation restoration uses the same capacity gate. Four cohomology and two-group examples now complete on the VM, and exhausting the configured ceiling still exits with FRAME OVERFLOW.

  • WebGPU fast-tier admission reports the actual gate (SW-272). A fast backend with the default 1e-9 tolerance refused dispatch but described itself as opted in. Admission and diagnostics now share the same precision check and report the required gateTolerance >= 1e-6. Browser WASM table callbacks use the shared JSPI wrapper in both loaders.

  • Shared browser WebGPU backends keep concurrent VMs isolated (SW-270). An asynchronous readback could write through another VM’s memory, yielding zeros in the first VM’s matmul result. Each operation now retains its own destination context. Reported by Colin with hardware reproductions.

  • Inexact tensor division follows IEEE 754 across engines (SW-271). The VM and native broadcast helper previously returned zero for a zero denominator, while native same-shape codegen and WebGPU returned signed infinity or NaN. Reported by Colin with hardware reproductions.

  • Language-coverage determinism runs now create their source corpus under the checkout for direct invocation, CTest, and the release coverage runner (SW-252). The display-path writer correctly shortened files in a system temporary directory to their basename, which the gate could not reopen relative to the checkout; both JIT and AOT then failed without checking any source locations. The gate still requires readable sources, in-range locations, and identical records across runs.

  • Added Colin’s requested bounded WGSL strain-energy artifact example: a native/VM-verified AD generator, sealed manifest and f32 evaluator, and a Chrome three-kernel resident-buffer demo with measured error evidence.

  • The GPU suite now requires a terminal named PASS, rejects any FAIL marker, counts hardware absence as SKIP, and grades the GPU-versus-CPU differential rather than a single execution of its workload (SW-257). A planted-failure self-test covers bare and indented failures, incomplete output, and false differential passes. CUDA Ozaki checks now skip without a live CUDA backend.

  • The v1.3.5 recursive Taylor carrier also covers the unmerged AD carrier work from 172c796d7: nested complex derivatives, exact scalar Hessians, Taylor and tensor identities, parallel carrier transfer, and region evacuation agree on native and VM. The existing SW-193/SW-194 regressions and the level-carrier tests cover these cases without restoring the retired hyper-dual companion lanes (ADR-0027).

  • On the bytecode VM, tensor-sum/-mean/-max/-min with no axis now reduce the whole tensor to a number, as on native (SW-202). The VM used to answer #(0.75) for (tensor-sum (tensor 0.5 0.25)) and per-row sums for a matrix, because “no axis” and “last axis” were both -1; gpu-reduce had the same problem.

  • VM max/min reductions now start from the infinities, so the max of an all -inf.0 tensor is -inf.0, as on native and on the GPU path, not -1.797e308 (SW-246).

  • The VM’s GPU reduction path read an axis of -1 as “all axes” while its CPU path reads the last axis, so a matrix reduction changed answer at the GPU threshold (SW-243). The GPU now serves a reduction only when it computes the same one as the CPU path.

  • The VM’s GPU elementwise path (SW-245) required only equal element counts, so [6] + [1,6] would have produced a [6] result on a GPU backend instead of the broadcast [1,6]. It now requires identical shapes, and the VM’s GPU size test is the backend’s eshkol_gpu_should_use() instead of a private copy of the threshold.

  • The browser WASM glue now implements eshkol_tensor_shape_total, eshkol_matmul_shape_valid and eshkol_unwrap_list_index with their native contracts, returns the caller’s arena from eshkol_ad_home_arena (the glue never records a tape) and treats eshkol_enforce_tensor_elements as the native no-limit path, instead of throwing. web/eshkol-repl.js gains the real tagged i128 predicate the site runtime already had. check_wasm_imports.py compiles a reshape/matmul surface so these imports stay covered.

  • Preserve browser VM output without a trailing newline, including UTF-8 text. Learn and Examples no longer lose their last displayed result or carry it into a later evaluation. The Pages gate executes all 41 runnable site examples with output/property checks and no-newline, Unicode, and isolation regressions. (#712)

  • Restore website rendering by implementing the browser runtime’s tagged i128 predicate. Ordinary heap operands no longer throw during homepage startup; the Pages workflow now exercises this runtime import before deployment. (#711)

  • The dev REPL’s glue still threw on the same predicate. The site runtime fix above landed only in site/static/eshkol-runtime.js; web/eshkol-repl.js kept the old throwing stub for eshkol_is_i128_tagged, which generic arithmetic asks of every heap operand — so any REPL evaluation touching a pair, string or vector through generic arithmetic aborted with an uncaught WASM-glue exception. Ported the real implementation into the REPL glue so both files answer identically again.

  • Refreshed the homepage’s declared-surface and parity-row figures in site/src/main.esk, which had drifted behind tests/vm_parity/PARITY.tsv and tests/coverage/coverage_policy.json, and rebuilt site/static/eshkol-site.wasm so the deployed page carries the correction.

Added

  • Private, experimental ESKM v2 preflight validation with exact-byte fixtures, C and C++ consumers, and malformed-input checks. Public checkpoint I/O remains ESKM v1. (#699)

  • Navier-Stokes residual mechanization trajectory. Added docs/design/NAVIER_STOKES_RESIDUAL_MECHANIZATION.md, a step-by-step map from the 2026 OpenAI Navier-Stokes similarity-profile analysis to Eshkol primitives: 84 numbered computational rows following the paper’s own structure (similarity coordinates, cumulative radial moments, the admissible stress cone, heat exterior and analytic axis profiles, order-by-order background correction, auxiliary torus, the two-family stress solve, the residual ladder, and localization to a compactly supported force), each with the Eshkol builtin or library module that performs it or the build item that will, a version, and a verification gate. The accompanying examples/mathematics_navier_stokes_{viscosity_scaling,similarity_scales,pulse_stress,first_principles}.esk programs run the four steps that are executable today — the exact viscosity-scaling identity through the AD residual operator, the similarity exponents as an exactly solved rational system, the leading-order profile balance by Taylor-coefficient collection with a negative control, and the pulse momentum-flux averages with the two-family stress solve — and are discovered by the existing examples suite. ROADMAP.md gains the corresponding capability entries under v1.4.0-connection, v1.5.0-intelligence, v1.6.0-reasoning and v1.7.0-synthesis. (#625, #627, #629)

  • core.exact_linalg: exact rational linear algebra and torus averaging. Added a pure-Scheme library module — exact-matrix?, exact-matrix-ref, exact-matrix-mul, exact-matrix-transpose, exact-det (fraction-free Bareiss elimination), exact-solve, exact-inverse, exact-rank, exact-nullspace, torus-average and torus-average-2d — operating on plain vectors-of-vectors over the scalar exact tower (int64/bignum/exact rational), never tensors, so results stay exact under R7RS numeric contagion whenever every input does. torus-average/torus-average-2d weight discrete samples on a circle / T² grid by an always-exact 1/n / 1/(n*m). Supports the auxiliary-torus and two-family stress-solve steps of the Navier-Stokes residual mechanization trajectory above. (#632)

  • AI-driven mathematics examples. Added four pure Eshkol programs that exactly verify public finite witnesses: the 2026 Jacobian-conjecture counterexample and its fiber geometry, AlphaTensor rank-23 and rank-47 matrix-multiplication factorizations over F2, and the FunSearch 512-cap in AG(8,3). The examples are discovered by the existing examples test suite and document the exact arithmetic and AD verification boundaries. (#570)

  • ADR-0000 Stage 1, phase A: the frontend node-identity substrate. Every AST node the parser produces now carries a stable NodeId, and a side table maps that id to a SourceSpan — the first column of the NodeId -> {SourceSpan, BindingId, TypedExprInfo} substrate that ADR-0000 §7 risk 1 calls “the single most important co-design constraint in the whole program”, and that ADRs 0004, 0006 and 0008 all have to be built on if the compiler, LSP, docs, REPL and VM are to give one answer rather than five.

    New: inc/eshkol/frontend/node_identity.h (the API), lib/frontend/node_identity.cpp (a chunked, lock-free-read span table), eshkol_ast_t::node_id (the 4-byte key; the payload stays out of line, per ADR-0008 §4.2), and tests/frontend/node_identity_test.cpp.

    The parser’s 32 location-stamping sites now write the location and the identity in one statement, so the two cannot drift apart, and the stream reader closes each top-level form’s span with a measured extent — the first place in the frontend that records where a construct ends rather than only where it begins.

    NodeIds are tagged, not bare indices, because eshkol_ast_t is declared uninitialised in a dozen places (macro_expander.cpp, sexp_to_ast.cpp, introspection.cpp) and an unset field holds garbage. A garbage word is rejected by its tag and again by its bound, so it reads as “unknown” — the same discipline source_file_id already follows, for the same reason: a diagnostic may fail to name a location, but it must never name a wrong one confidently.

    Strictly additive. line, column and source_file_id keep their exact previous values and every consumer that has not moved onto the substrate reads what it always read. (#476)

  • The LLVM codegen dispatcher resolves diagnostics through the substrate. codegenAST asks NodeId -> SourceSpan for the file, line and column it reports, falling back to the node’s own fields when the substrate does not know a node (one synthesized after parsing — an import lowered to a define alias, a macro expansion). This is the first real consumer, and it is the diagnostics path deliberately: it is where ESH-0364 (a location naming the wrong file) and ESH-0365 (a location on the closing paren) both landed.

    Emitted locations are unchanged — the span was recorded from the same token that set line/column. What changes is that a node’s file no longer depends on the traversal that reached it: source_file_id is stamped only on top-level forms and every inner node borrowed the ambient context, whereas in the span table each stamped node carries its own file. ScopedAstProvenance gained the integer fast path its own comment always claimed, now that it runs per node instead of per top-level form. (#476)

  • scripts/run_node_identity_gate.py measures substrate coverage. With ESHKOL_NODE_IDENTITY_STATS=1 every frontend process prints eshkol-node-identity: allocated=N queried=N resolved=N located=N extent=N as it exits. The gate compiles a mixed corpus, aggregates, and emits node_identity_substrate_present and node_identity_span_coverage as ICC runtime_event traces against a monotonic floor in tests/coverage/NODE_IDENTITY_BASELINE.json. Graded by the new adr0000-s1-identity completion oracle.

    The gated number is measured at a consumer, not at the parser: a parser-side count says only how many ids were minted, never whether the answer arrived where it was needed. “Has an identity”, “has a location” and “has an extent” stay three separate numbers so none can be read as another. This is what makes the stage falsifiable, which is the whole point of ADR-0000’s gates.

    Phase A only, and the oracle says so: BindingId (ADR-0006 slices 1-2), TypedExprInfo (ADR-0004 spine part 1), the one semantic tooling core (ADR-0008 M0/M1 — the LSP still counts parentheses by hand), byte-offset canonical spans and the expansion-origin table are all still outstanding, and none of them is half-built here. (#476)

  • The bytecode VM reclaims memory: a Stage-1 OALR region evacuator ports with-region reclamation to the VM heap (SW-14, the v1.3.5 flagship item). (with-region ...) used to lower to begin on the VM. The body ran, its value was returned, and not one byte came back: measured on tests/memory/vm_region_growth_watchdog_test.esk, peak RSS was 1.503 GB with the wrapper and 1.504 GB without it — the same to within 0.06%. The form was inert, not merely weak, and the VM had no heap reclamation of any kind.

    It now reclaims, and the claim is measured rather than asserted. The same fixture, swept by iteration count: 26 MB at 1 000 iterations, 26 MB at 4 000, 26 MB at 16 000 — sixteen times the work for two megabytes — against 796 MB for the identical program on the identical binary with ESHKOL_VM_REGION_EVAC=0. Gated by tests/memory/vm_region_flat_rss_test.sh, which requires the flat curve, the on/off separation, and the printed answer to be identical either way.

    The port matches the native engine’s semantics, not its implementation. Native copies the escaping subgraph (Cheney-style, forwarding map, mutation write barrier at every store into a longer-lived slot). The VM marks from its root set and sweeps at arena-block granularity instead, because a VM value addresses the heap by a small integer index rather than by pointer: a copying evacuator there would have to rewrite indices, and a missed rewrite aliases a live object and returns a wrong value rather than crashing — the failure mode the SW-14 ruling called strictly worse than the leak. Marking moves nothing, so eq? identity, shared structure and cycles survive without special handling. Live objects’ fixed-size headers are copied one level out, which needs no layout knowledge because the object table is the only holder of that address.

    Coverage is total by construction. vm_evac_subtype_table[] classifies the full 33-wide heap tag space — the 28 HeapType members plus the three tags vm_geometric.c defines outside the enum as bare macros and the two unassigned slots — with a compile-time span check, a fatal startup check that every row is filled in, and a default: arm that pins the region rather than guessing. That width is the ESH-0214d lesson applied up front: a switch over the enum alone would have fallen through for the manifold tags exactly as evac_kind_for once fell through for KB/FACT/SUBSTITUTION/WORKSPACE. tests/memory/vm_region_evac_subtype_coverage_test.esk builds one live instance of every subtype a program can construct inside a region, escapes it, drops 600 regions’ worth of sibling garbage and reads each one back to compare its contents — under ESHKOL_ARENA_POISON=1 (dead blocks stamped 0xCB and kept mapped, retired indices never recycled), under an audit that independently scans the object table for a surviving reference to a retired index, and with reclamation disabled.

    A raise crossing a region and a continuation transfer out of one go through the same teardown call as normal exit, so the structured and unstructured surfaces cannot drift apart — the discipline native keeps around eshkol_region_unwind_to().

    Every case the evacuator is not certain about degrades toward the leak and never toward a dangling index: an unclassified subtype, a continuation captured inside a region, or a failed bookkeeping allocation all pin the region, which is then promoted whole.

  • Assurance wave 1: ledger-integrity and oracle-schema gates with self-tests (#454). scripts/check_ledger_integrity.py fails .icc/silent-wrong-ledger.yaml on a parse error, a duplicate id across the file, or an entry missing a required field or closure evidence (SW-33/SW-35/SW-42 were each independently double- allocated across branches, invisible to a textual merge). scripts/check_oracle_schema.py fails .icc/completion-oracles.yaml on a parse error or a structurally invalid criterion, and always reports declared-vs-graded criteria counts per oracle. scripts/gate_no_silent_wrong.py gains --self-test. All three wired into the eshkol-compiler-readiness oracle, added as ctest entries, and run in a new assurance-gates CI job. See docs/TESTING.md.

  • R7RS 7.1.1 vertical-line symbol syntax: read and write (#462). <identifier> -> <vertical line> <symbol element>* <vertical line> is one of R7RS’s three <identifier> productions; Eshkol previously implemented only the other two, so '|weird sym| lexed as two separate tokens. All four readers (native tokenizer, VM tokenizer, native runtime read, VM runtime read) now accept the full <symbol element> alphabet, including the mnemonic escapes, \|, and \x<hex>;. The bars request a verbatim spelling (#!fold-case does not apply inside them), and |.| is an ordinary symbol distinct from the bare . dotted-pair delimiter. write emits bars only when a name cannot be spelled bare under the R7RS grammar; display never bars. Shared predicate/escaper in inc/eshkol/core/symbol_syntax.h keeps the native and VM writers byte-identical. New regression: tests/features/pipe_symbol_test.esk (51 checks), run on native JIT/AOT/VM as a three-way parity check. See Complete Language Specification §2.1.6.

  • Multi-shot, re-entrant call/cc on native JIT, native AOT and the bytecode VM. A captured continuation can now be invoked any number of times, from any dynamic extent, including after the procedure that captured it has already returned — the shape generators, coroutines and amb-style backtracking search all need, and previously crashed (SIGILL/SIGSEGV, native) or hung (bytecode VM) the moment a program tried it. Native gives a capture that may outlive its frame a durable copy of the live C stack, restored to the same addresses before longjmping, so every interior pointer — frame pointers, spilled registers, addresses of locals held by closures, the jmp_buf itself — stays valid with no relocation; an escape-only capture (the common case: early return, exception-style unwinding) is unaffected and keeps the original zero-overhead setjmp/longjmp path. The VM snapshots its own operand stack and call-frame array, excluding top-level bindings (the store) from the control snapshot R7RS actually asks call/cc to capture, so set!/define effects at top level survive re-entry. dynamic-wind reroots on both engines: re-entering a continuation captured inside a dynamic-wind whose extent has since been left re-runs that extent’s before thunk, per R7RS 6.10. Gated on all three engines by scripts/run_continuation_tests.sh against six fixtures in tests/continuations/. Two known limits remain, both tracked in .icc/silent-wrong-ledger.yaml: a binding established after capture on the VM’s operand-stack store (refused with a diagnostic, not silently corrupted — SW-61), and a non-boxed set!-assigned local rolled back on re-entry on both engines pending assignment conversion (SW-62). See docs/reference/language/continuations.md. (#491)

  • A continuation captured inside with-region pins the region on native, matching the bytecode VM (SW-59). The VM already gave a region a real pin (heap_region_pin_all()) the moment a continuation was captured inside one; native had no analogue, so a continuation captured inside a with-region and resumed after that region exited read the region’s freed, poison-filled arena — a real use-after-free invisible to the non-poisoned test oracle (SIGSEGV at 0xcbcbcbcbcbcbcbcb under ESHKOL_ARENA_POISON=1, 3/3, both native JIT and AOT). eshkol_region_t now carries a pinned flag; capturing a continuation while any region is open (eshkol_make_continuation_state) pins every currently-open region (eshkol_region_pin_all(), mirroring heap_region_pin_all() exactly, including its permanence — neither engine ever unpins, since a continuation carries no refcount to unpin against); region_destroy() leaks a pinned region’s arena instead of freeing it, so the failure direction is a leak, never a dangle, on both engines identically. The fix is confined to region_destroy() — the single teardown path (eshkol_region_unwind_to(), covering lexical with-region exit, region-close, a raise crossing the region, and a call/cc escape) needed no changes, so pinning triggers only on an actual capture: a program that never calls call/cc shows zero behavior change, confirmed by the unchanged flat-RSS gates (tests/memory/region_mutating_loop_flat_rss_test.sh, tests/memory/define_loop_flat_rss_aot_test.sh, tests/memory/vm_region_flat_rss_test.sh). tests/continuations/region_capture_resume.esk is 3/3 clean under ESHKOL_ARENA_POISON=1 on all three engines, up from crashing on two of them. (#491)

  • scripts/check_ps1_encoding.py guards every tracked PowerShell script against BOM-less non-ASCII bytes. Verified on a real Windows PowerShell 5.1 host: a .ps1 file with two non-ASCII bytes and no byte-order mark parsed clean under CI’s pwsh 7 (UTF-8 by default) but threw 18 cascading parse errors on 5.1, which falls back to decoding a BOM-less script in the process’s system ANSI code page — corrupting string-literal boundaries around every non-ASCII byte. CI running the same bytes under a different default encoding could not have caught this by execution; the new gate checks the files’ own bytes instead, failing on any tracked *.ps1/ *.psm1 file that contains a byte >= 0x80 unless it opens with the UTF-8 BOM (EF BB BF), and reporting the exact file:line:col and codepoint of every offender. Self-tests (--self-test) cover the ASCII-only, BOM’d-UTF-8 and BOM-less-non-ASCII cases plus exact-location reporting; wired into the assurance-gates CI job and the eshkol-compiler-readiness oracle (ps1_encoding_clean). (#503)

  • The parser has no recursion budget: recursive descent is replaced by an explicit continuation stack. A child parse suspends into a heap-allocated C++20 coroutine frame and is resumed through a linked continuation stack, so await_suspend only records a child and final_suspend only suspends — neither resumes the other. Native stack consumption is therefore independent of grammar nesting, and stays independent in an unoptimized build. The driver covers expressions, list special forms, quote and quasiquote, vectors, string interpolation, types, match patterns, syntax-rules patterns, import sets and feature requirements; exceptions propagate back through suspended parents, destroying each completed child. Lambda capture collection became an explicit pending-node vector, and type parsing attaches arena-owned child trees rather than copying subtrees, which removes a quadratic type allocation. Two downstream passes that used to re-introduce the dependency — TypeChecker::synthesize/synthesizeApplication and the codegenAST → codegenOperation → codegenCall → codegenArithmetic → codegenTypedAST chain — run on the same driver, extracted unchanged into inc/eshkol/util/continuation_task.h. Gated by parser_explicit_stack (16,000 levels of nesting on an actual 8 MiB pthread stack under an 8 MiB process resource limit) and parser_stack_compile (both the soft and hard process stack limits fixed at 8 MiB, 16,000 nested additions through JIT and AOT), on Linux x64 and macOS ARM64 in CI. On Darwin the gate rewrites LC_MAIN’s stack reservation to 8 MiB and re-signs, because RLIMIT_STACK cannot shrink the main mapping there, and sets ESHKOL_JIT_CACHE=0 so the JIT arm exercises in-process ORC rather than an AOT cache hit. The 65,536-byte safety margin and every production stack limit are unchanged. This is a claim about the parse-and-lower chain: specialized backend helpers outside it keep their synchronous entry points. Reported in docs/reports/PARSER_EXPLICIT_STACK_20260908.md and docs/reports/PARSER_JIT_STACK_20260908.md.

  • Deep non-tail user recursion reports a diagnostic instead of dying on a guard page (ESH-0101, ESH-0112, ledger SW-81). Exhausting the stack now prints eshkol: stack overflow …, names the stack size and ESHKOL_STACK_SIZE, and exits nonzero — on JIT, AOT and inside parallel-map workers, each of which gets its own sigaltstack. A larger ESHKOL_STACK_SIZE completes the same program. The former SIGILL was the guard-page trap with no handler installed.

  • Deeply nested non-macro expressions compile in near-linear time and memory (ESH-0103). The superlinear frontend path is gone, with a bounded JIT and AOT compile-time and RSS ladder at 1,000 / 4,000 / 16,000 levels asserting near-linear scaling in CI.

  • One exhaustive operation dispatcher and one callable dispatcher. inc/eshkol/core/ast_routing.h owns a runtime switch over all 113 eshkol_op_t members; each consumer supplies explicit operation groups, and template instantiation requires every operation exactly once. Every callable path enters codegenClosureCall. Evidence ships with it: 14 structural mutation controls, complete-domain dispatch for all 113 operations, and 14 callable assertions under both JIT and AOT covering derivatives, captures, variadics, parameter procedures, macros, parallel workers, mutable captures and apply tail recursion.

  • (tensor-apply tensor callable) calls the callable, not the name. Both operands are evaluated once and the resolved callable is invoked on each scalar in row-major order; no function-name table and no identity substitution participates, so a lexical binding or user definition that shadows a historical builtin name determines what runs, and a non-callable raises even for an empty tensor. Native routes through the same codegenClosureCall an ordinary lambda application uses; the VM routes through the single vm_enter_call entry shared by threaded OP_CALL, switch OP_CALL and native higher-order invocation. The scalar load is the one tensor indexing already uses, so reverse-mode node pointers and complete forward jets — Taylor coefficients and captured differentiable values included — survive the call. Gated by tensor_apply_callable_matrix: LLVM JIT at O0, LLVM AOT at O2, VM source execution and emitted ESKB must each report all 31 ordered assertions, with JIT caching disabled. Design note: docs/design/TENSOR_APPLY_CALLABLE_DISPATCH.md.

  • Dense tensor autodiff executes (ledger SW-48). matmul, tensor-sum and tensor-mean each record exactly one dense AD node under differentiation, and that node is what matmul returns. The reverse pass had keyed on tensor_gradient, which is null at record time, so a tensor node fell into the scalar dispatch and dereferenced the input1/input2 pointers a tensor node legitimately leaves null; it now discriminates on tensor_value or tensor_gradient. Under AD, matmul returns the AD_NODE_MATMUL node tagged CALLABLE with the AD-node subtype, and outside AD mode returns the plain tensor unchanged. A new registry row AD_NODE_TENSOR_PACK bridges scalarized operands by identity scatter; it performs no arithmetic, which is why the scalar and dense lowerings agree exactly rather than closely. Dense elementwise arithmetic uses the same shape-aware dispatcher and accumulates repeated indices for broadcast operands. scripts/run_dense_tensor_ad_gate.sh gates the two lowerings against each other for byte-identical gradients; tests/ad/matmul_tape_node_count_test.esk ratchets the recorded node count; and tests/ad/dense_tensor_ad_gradcheck_test.esk asserts that the per-op cost of a 6×6 matmul equals that of a 2×2 once the gradient driver’s per-input variable nodes are subtracted. ADR-0002 records the resolution.

  • Forward-mode duals survive the neural primitives (SW-111). Layer norm, scaled-dot attention and tensor-get carry duals on both engines: tensor coercion accepts dual-bearing vectors, scalarized AD tensor elements are no longer decoded as plain f64, and the VM gains parallel dual buffers and the transformer operations. Runtime, statically typed and densified tensor activations all route through the AD path and preserve their runtime carriers.

  • Exact-coefficient Taylor and user-number AD are gated together. The acceptance the claim needs had never actually run: four of its twelve registrations did not exist, because tests/ad/ was not globbed by run_autodiff_tests.sh. Exact-coefficient 68/68 and reverse-over-Taylor 18/18 now run together, with the VM returning exact derivatives for exact inputs through exact rational dual halves and a K+1 Taylor carrier. Residuals closed alongside: the VM cosine Taylor recurrence sign, abs on exact values, bignum and rational exactness, signed powers, and Taylor dispatch for max, ReLU, sigmoid and tanh. Hermetic Taylor gate 12/12; the exact suite is 159/159 bit-identical between native and VM.

  • (derivative (lambda (x) (* x x)) 1/3) returns 2/3 on the VM (SW-85). Exactness was lost at the seed: VmDual was two doubles, so every AD entry read the point through as_number_vm(). Only exact? separated the two answers, which is why every value-comparing differential passed.

  • Nested differentiation composes. Outer-epoch perturbations are carried through inner passes on a shared epoch-aware Taylor projection with an exact tangent sidecar whose layout is defined and which survives evacuation. A foreign-epoch tower is opaque with respect to the current value recurrence rather than flattened to a constant, so a closure-captured outer tower cannot be silently erased by an inner pass. Cross-engine gate 6/6, reviewer fixture 29/29 on JIT, AOT and VM with byte-identical transcripts, remaining compositions 5/5.

  • A Hessian over a lambda that captures its enclosing parameter is analytic (native JIT and AOT 16/16, VM parity 3/3), generic i128 arithmetic and comparison work on both engines (57/57), and a native tape mark/release API lets a bare resident training loop reclaim each reverse tape without a region — measured at +0 bytes over 200 passes (LE-02, LE-03, SW-93).

  • Squared distance is a real AD node with bridge backwards for Euclidean, hyperbolic, spherical and product manifolds, with signed-curvature dispatch (K<0 hyperbolic, K=0 Euclidean, K>0 spherical), finite-input and on-manifold validation, stable asinh, antipode detection with no absolute cutoff and overflow-safe weighting. Gradcheck 45/45.

  • The four geometric bridge operators have exact backward rules (SW-65). ad_hyperbolic_distance, ad_poincare_exp_map, ad_poincare_log_map and ad_geodesic_attention return real gradients where every input previously received exactly 0.0 with no diagnostic and exit 0. The exp and log rules reuse FrechetGeometry::mobius_add_with_jacobians and log_map_with_jacobians rather than re-deriving them, and ad_geodesic_attention retains its softmax weights and records causality and curvature so the mask and metric are reconstructible at backward time. Node types with no backward are now explicit: TANGENT_PROJECT, MOBIUS_ADD, MOBIUS_MATMUL and GYROVECTOR_SPACE are declared UNREGISTERED and abort naming themselves rather than propagating silence (#498).

  • Forward AD-node producers for ad_tensor_embedding and ad_frechet_mean, so the embedding and Fréchet-mean backward rules are exercised by real recorded nodes instead of hand-assembled fixtures. Fractional, negative and out-of-range embedding indices are refused at record time on the same criterion the backward uses, and the Fréchet producer refuses to record a node when the Karcher iteration has not converged. The VM’s f64 Karcher iteration moved into inc/eshkol/backend/frechet_mean_core.h so the opcode and the differentiable path cannot drift (#497).

  • The no-finite-differences guarantee is falsifiable. eshkol_ad_count_fd() shipped with zero callers, so (= (ad-finite-difference-evals) 0) proved nothing. A write end exists on both engines — (ad-note-finite-difference!) — and record-fd-op! reports one evaluation per perturbation, so the count equals the extra primal evaluations paid. A negative control drives the counter to exactly 4 over two inputs (#474).

  • A structural AD carrier gate (.icc/ad-carrier-manifest.yaml with scripts/gate_ad_shared_node_model.py) re-derives from source, per substrate, which differentiation carrier answers each AD operator and whether it is exact: seven checks including bidirectional coverage against tests/vm_parity/PARITY.tsv, the rule that a finite difference may never back a vm-supported operation, and a shrink-only ratchet on the carrier fork (#487).

  • The constant-curvature geometry has one implementation. qllm_bridge.cpp calls the shared riemannian_core.h primitives for distance, the exponential and logarithmic maps and geodesic attention, and qLLM interop binds to the real private ABI through cmake/QllmBridge.cmake and lib/bridge/qllm_interop.cpp with its own gate rather than a re-implementation. Bridge operations refuse off-manifold inputs and branch on the sign of curvature instead of clamping toward the boundary, which had fabricated finite log-map magnitudes; geodesic attention gains flat and spherical branches and validates every row before scoring instead of emitting NaN (SW-76).

  • Reverse geometry rules are derived from the shared scaled forward. The log coincidence limit is exact, spherical forward and reverse share one domain, attention is overflow-safe and finite-checked, and a spherical derivative request beyond f64 is refused explicitly rather than answered. Curvature series use a degree-10 branch in q = K r² / 4 with cancellation-free derivatives, a binary128 witness, an identified switch point and a 1,067-binade sweep; the curvature Hessian is exponent-separated across all binades and adjoints are exponent-scaled at subnormal curvature. Dual-versus-finite-difference gradcheck 131 cases. Centered softmax backward gives exactly 0 for uniform scores.

  • Near-boundary Poincaré-ball arithmetic is computed to full relative precision: a double-double dot closed with FMA for 1 − B|x|² and 1 + B⟨x,y⟩, the Möbius denominator via Lagrange’s identity (a sum of squares, exactly zero for collinear vectors) instead of a cancelling difference, and the numerator from two-product and two-sum. The 1e-15 floor the cancelling form appeared to require is removed.

  • Antipode detection is exact, with no tolerance band: a canonicalized normalized dot product shared by native and VM, lossless power-of-two scaling, then exact dyadic mantissa and exponent comparison in place of floating product and FMA residuals. Validated by a 4.16-million-case exact-rational sweep.

  • Riemannian Adam is intrinsic and transactional. An intrinsic per-factor second moment, state committed only after both the exponential map and the transport succeed, and the invariant that the first step’s geodesic distance from the start is exactly the learning rate on the Euclidean, ball and sphere branches — the coordinate-wise construction gives lr·√n and fails for every n > 1. The implicit state pool is VM-owned and doubles from 16 rather than falling back to slot 0 for a seventeenth shape and silently zeroing an earlier shape’s moments; an allocation failure raises a catchable error. The regression drives 20 distinct shapes through and compares against an independently maintained explicit-state trajectory with varying gradients, because Adam’s bias correction hides clobbering under a constant gradient.

  • curl returns the correct result on every documented field shape (LE-12). A field written (lambda (v) (list …)) returning a cons cell no longer reads car and cdr words as tensor dimensions and an element pointer. A new eshkol_list_to_svec_raw preserves AD nodes instead of coercing components to 0.0, and native and VM resolve field arity by the same rule as gradient — arity 1 takes the whole point, arity N takes N spread scalars — so op:CURL enters the cross-engine differential (#518).

  • Vector-parameter AD over captured locals is exact on native JIT, native AOT and the VM across the vref and vector-ref spellings, captured let bindings, two captures in one lambda, inert captures, builder reuse and curl: 23 new checks, plus tests/ad/sweep_c_regressions_test.esk registered in CTest so its five regressions actually run (LE-06, #519).

  • exterior-derivative and hodge-star are real operators on a faithful differential-form representation — a k-form on Rⁿ known to jet order r, carried over the lexicographic increasing-multi-index basis — where they previously refused as unimplemented, because coefficient values do not determine d and C(n,k) = C(n,n−k) leaves k ambiguous. curvature-gradient, curvature-hessian and adaptive-curvature-step are derivatives of the documented objective rather than heuristics, and parallel transport uses the linear gyration closed form. Regression assertions go from 42 to 98, including d∘d = 0 checked with = rather than a tolerance, *(dx∧dy) = dz, the n=4/k=1 involution sign, and a conformal Poincaré metric where the star scales by 8/3.

  • The DD-10 documented no-op surface is implemented rather than documented down (SW-105, SW-106). -D NAME[=VALUE] exposes cond-expand features, -fPIC documents and exercises native AOT position-independent emission, provide privacy is enforced on native JIT, native AOT and the VM, and gyro-transport uses closed-form gyrovector transport with VM parity coverage. Module privacy is scope-aware on all engines, with hygienic private capture in exported macros, VM load, and nested cond-expand.

  • One deterministic PRNG sequence per seed across native JIT, native AOT and the VM (SW-113). Native code emitted libc drand48/srand48 while the JIT and runtime used the Eshkol wrappers, and an explicit srand48 did not suppress time-based reseeding, so a fixed seed gave different sequences under AOT and JIT. All three engines share the canonical runtime PRNG, the VM gains random, srand48, set-random-seed! and tensor rand, and cross-engine tests pin the first eight outputs per seed plus reseeding, negative and low-32-bit seeds. (#554)

  • One string-escape grammar for four readers. The frontend tokenizer, VM source parser, VM runtime datum reader and hosted runtime datum reader share inc/eshkol/core/string_escape.h, so named escapes, bounded one-to-three-digit octal, R7RS \xHEX;, documented \uNNNN, line continuations and embedded NUL bytes diagnose identically on every engine (#569).

  • The runtime reader handles quasiquote, unquote and unquote-splicing. eshkol_read_sexpr(), shared by native JIT, AOT and the REPL, special-cased only ' and (; backquote, comma and comma-at fell through to the atom reader, so (read (open-input-string "\(a ,b)“))returned the one-character symbol `` `and left(a ,b)` unconsumed to corrupt the next read on the same port — a silent wrong datum with no diagnostic and exit 0. All four readers now agree, cross-checked against the frontend parser’s own quoted-data output on byte-identical text, over nesting, vector literals, adjacent delimiters and two stream-desync guards.

  • UTF-8 preserves NUL bytes and validates character ranges across engines. Header byte lengths for full native conversion, character-based substring bounds for optional ranges, and invalid strings, index types and ranges rejected before allocation or copying. unicode.cpp moved into the shared runtime-core source set so native, VM and ESKB Unicode output align — parity 228/228.

  • Assignment conversion boxes every set!-assigned local on native and the VM (SW-62, PR-01/04/09), so captured continuations observe assignments correctly. It ships with a parser-owned binding-form registry and a completeness self-test, scope-aware mutation for named-let, guards, define-values, shadowing, initializers, letrec* and linear boxes, and a shared table-driven observed-after-mutation analysis for native and VM including guard handlers, with a seven-form parity regression. The engine-parity baseline is remeasured over 219 programs with seven named dispositions.

  • letrec and letrec* patch each upvalue from the slot the closure actually captured. The second pass had walked closure and binding lists in lockstep, which only holds when a closure captures every sibling in declaration order from the first, so mutual is-even?/is-odd? answered (#t #t) on the VM against (#t #f) native. letrec* got no patching at all, on the reasoning that sequential initialization makes each binding final — but an initializer’s lambda may name a later binding, capturing the placeholder by value, and the VM then called NIL. Nesting shapes are pinned: a sibling captured from inside a let, an inner letrec inside an outer letrec’s initializer, and a lambda nested two deep.

  • A wrong-arity call to a raw builtin or a conversion intrinsic is refused on both engines, and the refusal reads the builtin’s real minimum rather than its opcode’s operand count. Native exact->inexact, inexact, numerator and denominator indexed argument zero with no count check; the VM’s generic call path never compared argument count to the builtin table’s arity, so (ceiling) compiled clean and printed 0 from an uninitialised local. A builtin’s minimum arity is a distinct concept from its operand count — hash-ref shares an opcode with three-argument hash-table-ref/default — so min_arity is added with three states, positive for a real minimum and negative for variadic, needed by exactly one of 732 entries plus the variadic gcd and lcm, and pinned by scripts/check_builtin_min_arity.py.

  • Order predicates are variadic with a minimum of two (R7RS 6.2.6). The direct-builtin arity table listed <, >, <=, >= and = as exactly two, which made the n-ary chain lowering unreachable and refused (<= 1 2 2 3 3).

  • A handler returning from a non-continuable raise raises a secondary exception on every engine (SW-84), per R7RS 6.11, carrying the original condition’s message — native codegen, the VM source path and ESKB, which gains OP_RAISE_SECONDARY. raise-continuable is unchanged.

  • Character predicates return canonical booleans and classify Unicode identically on both engines (SW-86). glibc’s isalpha/isdigit bitmasks are no longer stored as boolean payloads, so (eq? (char-alphabetic? c) #t) no longer fails on Linux while passing on macOS. Shared classifiers, fixed VM UTF-8 character-literal decoding, and a parity corpus over ASCII, Latin-1 and multibyte codepoints. (#539)

  • The VM loads the canonical standard library on the source, REPL and ESKB paths (PR-02, SW-99, SW-100), taking the unresolved-name baseline from 323 to 0: every native-resolvable public name resolves on the VM. The prelude cache is gated against the dispatch table, generated deterministically under the C locale, and compiles lib/stdlib.esk and its full require closure so the embedded WASM standard library matches native.

  • Closure capture is lossless at scale. Capture counts are encoded in a versioned ESKB with regressions at 255, 256, 257, 4,096 and 65,536 captures on VM, native and WASM; the hosted compiler gains dynamic capture storage and the same encoding-bound diagnostic on every engine; and the VM and WASM paths traverse every capture in evacuation and parallel paths. Parallel dispatch uses the dynamic environment ABI for all capture counts across map, for-each, execute and fold. Compiler and runtime share one upvalue capacity, ESHKOL_VM_MAX_CLOSURE_UPVALUES = 32, replacing a compiler cap of 32 against a runtime array of 16 — a procedure referencing 17 to 32 distinct top-level names used to strand values on the operand stack and corrupt the next top-level define. (#463)

  • map with runtime closures walks every list operand in lockstep through one shared MapCodegen::mapWithClosureN, stopping at the shortest list, with empty inputs handled.

  • Proper tail calls that musttail cannot take are unbounded. A tail-transfer dispatcher removes the differing-arity and non-AArch64 restrictions: three symbols per participating procedure, a per-thread transfer record and one driver frame per chain, with the public entry keeping its name, type, linkage and Function* identity. guard is refuted rather than fixed, and the two remaining shapes are scoped with their exact requirements (#483).

  • Mutual tail recursion is optimized in every R7RS 3.5 tail spelling — cond, case, when, unless, and the last operand of and and or — not only if. The walker now understands the same six forms as the oracle; previously such programs grew one native frame per hop and died between 500,000 and 5,000,000 hops while the byte-identical if spelling ran flat past 5,000,000 (#478).

  • Closed-enum dispatch is compiler-enforced. inc/eshkol/ad_node_registry.def is the single declaration point for an AD node type — name, value, payload kind, backward disposition — and the enum, the tensor dispatcher’s arms and the bridge backward table are generated from those rows under -Werror=switch-enum. A node type cannot exist without stating its backward disposition, a bridge row naming a nonexistent function fails the build, and a static assertion proves the enum stays dense. No default: can stand in for an invariant (#500).

  • Opt-in per-function AOT IR telemetry reports source, module, function, elapsed time and block and instruction counts, with an explicit unavailable status for the numbers the legacy emission placement cannot supply, so large-program build-time scaling can be measured rather than guessed (#571). The large-single-file AOT compile-time shape is measured and gated against a 900-second ceiling; growth is super-linear (#495).

  • Continuation region pins have a lifecycle (SW-74). A capture the compiler has already proven escape-only takes no pin, and a live pin promotes instead of leaking, so (with-region (call/cc (lambda (k) … (k v)))) in a loop no longer leaks a whole region arena per iteration for the life of the process. The VM enforces the pin budget by arena usage rather than by count. (#524)

  • A resident daemon loop no longer leaks through its guard (SW-49). eshkol_push_exception_handler bump-allocated each 40-byte frame from the shared REPL arena and popped it to nobody by explicit design: measured 11.98 MB at 10,000 ticks rising dead-linear to 449.56 MB at 1.6 million — 287.9 bytes per tick over a 160× span, with one, two and three guards per tick leaking 39.9, 73.4 and 120.4 bytes per tick. (#473)

  • file-stat returns six-field metadata with nanosecond mtime and a stable file identity on both engines; fs-write-atomic reports a failed final rename instead of returning Ok with no file; and SQLite TEXT binding is length-aware, so embedded NULs, empty TEXT, large TEXT and SQL NULL round-trip, with sqlite-column-type as the NULL discriminator (SW-130, SW-131, #564).

  • The agent surface handles real processes (SW-127, SW-128, SW-129): argv environment overlays and options on native and VM, multi-entry environments preserved, binary-safe subprocess reads with native lengths, tombstoned process handles with bounded spawning diagnostics, distinct terminal timeout, EOF and I/O-error statuses, and documented HTTP headers and SSE (#567). Hash-table and JSON round-trips are fixed at the root, with VM parity 109/109 (#566).

  • A quoted datum keeps its kind. parse_quoted_data_with_token() has a real branch for every legal datum token, so '#(), '#(1 2), backquote, comma and comma-at no longer fall through to the atom parser leaving their own tokens in the stream. (define (g x) (or x '#())) answering () for every input is closed, and unbalanced cases no longer swallow the rest of the file (#466).

  • Linear Qubit violations are compile-time errors, not warnings. Cloning a qubit no longer prints a warning, exits 0 and leaves a runnable binary. The verdict comes from a dedicated analyzeLinearUses() walk of the binder’s own scope rather than type-synthesis usage counters, which missed (begin (h q) (z q)) while wrongly rejecting (cond (b (h q)) (else (z q))) (#471).

  • core.memory’s store gains strict durable ownership (SW-97, IF-06): replay, compact tail state, close semantics, fsync plus parent-directory durability, and new memory-store-audit-linear and memory-store-linear-evidence procedures, with native JIT and AOT durability tests and a VM dependency parity fixture (#538).

  • assert-close and certify-kernel are documented public procedures in lib/core/testing.esk with their own test, rather than being defined inline by the GPU gate and the Ozaki certification test (#522).

  • Tensor and model loading validates its payload (SW-109). Loading is unified on validated ESKM v1 with magic, version, flags, shape, payload-size, overflow, full-consumption and CRC validation; corrupt payloads are rejected with diagnostics before materialization; legacy ESKT registrations that shadowed the validated loader are gone; missing files and invalid input ports are catchable errors on both engines; and the VM preflights ESKM records before materialization (#549, #555).

  • One validation layer for shape products, metadata, broadcasting and indexing, used by every tensor constructor and operation entry on native and VM, plus the adjacent defects a user meets next: attention shape relations beyond rank, the layer-norm parameter shape contract without modulo wrap, tensor-get bounds instead of aliasing, and a Box-Muller log(0) guard (SW-114). Shape validation is preserved at the VM and XLA boundaries, reshape enforces exact element counts, and empty shapes are preserved across engines.

  • Broadcast allocates the true broadcast size. The old path allocated the product of the two operand sizes capped at 16Mi and then wrote the result count: the product of operand sizes is not the broadcast size (that is the product of per-axis maxima), the cap overran the heap for any result above 16,777,216 elements, and the product could wrap int64 into a near-zero allocation followed by an enormous write. A new runtime entry eshkol_broadcast_shape_f64 computes the result shape.

  • Cross-entropy validates its targets and computes the documented loss (SW-112) for normalized-probability, one-hot and indexed forms on native, VM and the bridge AD, with shape, finiteness, normalization, integrality and range checks and a matching backward; the VM exposes cross-entropy-loss (#552, #633).

  • Checkpoint publication is atomic on validated ESKM routes. A complete temporary checkpoint is written in the destination directory and committed by rename, so a handled failure before the commit preserves the old destination. Documented in docs/design/ATOMIC_CHECKPOINT_SAVES.md as an atomic-replacement contract and explicitly not a power-loss durability guarantee.

  • ESKM v1 engine parity and an ESKT cross-reader matrix are release gates: a literal ESKM engine matrix executed across JIT, AOT, VM source and ESKB; a documented v1 compatibility corpus of reviewed fixtures; an ESKT four-engine cross-reader regression matrix with exact-byte negative controls; and an ICC oracle criterion for each (#596, #615). A deterministic ESKM fuzz gate is wired into release readiness; on Darwin, where setrlimit(RLIMIT_AS) always rejects a finite value, the harness gates both the call and the supports_limits flag that decides release-evidence eligibility, so a macOS run stops claiming a memory bound it cannot apply.

  • Object ABI v2, stages 1 and 2 (ADR-0012). A machine inventory of object-header layout dependence replaces a hand survey — 1,273 sites across 98 files on the semantic and emitted-IR layers, against 504 found by hand — and reaches three classes no grep can: intra-header field offsets baked into emitted LLVM IR, raw byte arithmetic naming neither the type nor an accessor, and cache keys omitting the object ABI entirely. A ratchet against .icc/abi-header-baseline.json grandfathers existing sites per class and per file and fails any commit adding one outside the accessor, with a self-test that proves the ratchet can go red. A link-time fingerprint guard makes a mixed-ABI link a link error rather than wrong answers at runtime, and persistent cache keys carry the ABI fingerprint, closing the second hazard with the same signature: a cached artifact is reused, not relinked, so no link-time check can fire. The WASM lane guards its geometry. Additive; no behavior change at v1 (#488, #528). The object-ABI tag is asserted through the documented --abi-fingerprint flag instead of a strings heuristic (#593), and compile_commands.json is exported whenever tests are built so the semantic ratchet always has its input (#578).

  • Eshkol compiles against LLVM 18 through 24. inc/eshkol/backend/llvm_compat.h carries the shims: createGlobalString for the removed CreateGlobalStringPtr; terminatorOrNull() for LLVM 24’s getTerminator() contract change, which the backend asks 137 times, so under 24 every guard fired and (define (add x y) (+ x y)) compiled to a call on two undefs with no return; and isSignatureValid for the LLVM 23+ spelling of the intrinsic check. terminatorOrNull is a function template so the LLVM 24 arm is never instantiated on older LLVM. The loop enable/disable hint is emitted in the single-operand form LLVM 23 onward requires — without it, 52 standard-library functions came out with empty bodies and the standard library never built. AArch64 instruction selection at O0 is no longer quadratic on large emitted functions.

  • Continuation IR operand lifetime is safe at the emission boundary. CodegenIRBuilder takes scalar GEP indices and call arguments by value and constructs LLVM’s borrowed ArrayRef views from named storage inside the synchronous emission method; a compiler-generated resume function had retained a view of a pre-suspension stack slot and discarded the factory’s return, producing a null GEP index. No compiler-version branches, flags or CI exclusions, and the regression fails its GEP operand-identity assertion if the raw LLVM builder is substituted back.

  • The XLA elementwise path broadcasts instead of reading past the smaller operand. The emitter loaded only the second operand’s data pointer while the runtime walked it with the first operand’s element count, so any broadcast overran the smaller operand once the pair crossed the dispatch threshold. The second operand’s shape now travels with it, equal shapes take the fast path, and everything else routes through the same exact-allocation broadcast runtime the CPU path uses. Separately, eshkol_xla_elementwise is now called with the ten arguments it takes: the declaration listed seven, so every emitted call passed the op-code into a later parameter’s slot and left three parameters reading argument registers — nothing crashed, which is why it survived.

  • Browser runtime imports are checked and complete. The WASM lane imports every runtime symbol through the JS environment object, so a new runtime function without a stub fails the macOS gate’s import check; the missing stubs are added, eshkol_broadcast_shape_f64 is declared in the import glue with validated outputs and its own test, and tensor shape total and validation are exported by the C VM module.

  • Metal soft-float f64 division is correct. Two independent defects: the restoring-division loop shifted left and set bit i in the same iteration, landing a bit set at iteration i at position 2i so most of the quotient mantissa fell off the register; and the integer conversion shifted the leading bit to bit 20 of the high word instead of bit 52 of the pair. This corrupted any Metal divide-by-integer, so (tensor-mean A axis) was wrong while (tensor-sum A axis) on the same tensor was exact. The regression compares three divisions over 400×400 f64 tensors for exact bit equality; a tolerance would have accepted the third case’s 0.27% error, and small powers of two never revealed it because their exact quotients have all-zero mantissas.

  • The GPU correctness gate can fail (SW-67). The gate printed unlabelled result lines and exited 0 unconditionally, and every other GPU test printed a FAIL: marker without ever reaching a nonzero exit code. Every GPU test now aggregates failures and exits nonzero if and only if a check failed, ten backend-independent self-check invariants give the gate teeth even run standalone, and tests/gpu/gate_canary_must_fail.esk is a permanent deliberate failure the runner requires to fail on every invocation (#501). The gate also runs as an advisory mesh lane on self-hosted CUDA labels (#522).

  • ld.lld is probed once and shared between the driver and the JIT link path on AArch64 Linux; without it, a warning and the default linker rather than an invalid-linker-name error on every link.

  • The compiler assurance framework measures its own gates against deliberate source mutations. scripts/run_compiler_assurance.py executes the production closed-enum gate twelve times in an isolated source projection — four repeats each at zero, one and two removed handwritten AD dispatcher case labels — and requires every baseline to pass, every treatment to be rejected, and finding counts to increase with dose by more than the within-dose range. It found an actual false green: a registry include had exempted all manual inline cases from missing-member checks. A --runtime-only lane exercises captured closures, case-lambda, parameter calls and apply with a capture against a hashed binary; gate_public_api_linkage.py generates a volatile function-pointer relocation per exported prototype from the umbrella header and requires it to compile, link and execute, covering 104 prototypes; and gate_compiler_architecture.py discovers AST operation switches, derives the operation set from eshkol_op_t and refuses omitted cases and defaults. Every run retains an evidence receipt — argv, streams, exit status, timing, source hash, gate hash, git revision. Documented in docs/platform/COMPILER_ASSURANCE.md, which also states the framework’s own limits: counts have resolution one, zero observed variation is measured repeatability rather than a claim about all machines, the mutation experiment measures the correctness gate and not a rebuilt compiler, and the policy is structural rather than a C++ semantic proof.

  • A self-verdict scanner catches the class where a test prints its own FAIL line and still exits 0. scripts/check_self_verdicts.py takes CTest or JUnit XML, a verdict manifest, or an ad hoc pair; drops zero-count summaries and banner titles; and its --self-test proves it goes red on a contrived contradiction. Ships with build fingerprints, adversarial scenarios and a readiness artifact probe (#465).

  • Every completion-oracle criterion is bound to a registered test and gated. Six criteria pointed at CTest patterns matching no test, and a no-match ctest -R exits 0. scripts/check_oracle_bindings.py fails CI when a selector matches zero tests or a referenced script is missing (#521). Separately, absent evidence no longer reads as ready: a target read ready/94 with zero evidence because its sole criterion was medium severity, which the grader degrades to a warning; criterion-level mediums across 14 other targets are swept to high or advisory, and scripts/audit_oracle_false_green.py fails any target with zero high-severity criteria (#472).

  • Harnesses distinguish infrastructure failure from code failure. scripts/lib/harness_outcome.sh supplies a signal-observing timeout wrapper that forks rather than execs, so a child’s own crash is never confused with our alarm; an exit-code classifier treating 124, 125, 127, 130, 137 and 142 as infrastructure; emitters that never publish a false verdict for an infrastructure outcome; and a retry-once helper. This closes the case where a 140-second cold-start JIT compile killed by a 60-second alarm reported FAIL and propagated into a dispatch-table completeness invariant (#475).

  • Failure attribution is explicit rather than a bare FAIL regex: a dedicated parser with a seven-case self-test wired into CI, and run_all_tests.sh rejecting stale bare-FAIL attribution, consuming named failure lists, supporting split crashes and deduplicating records across 46 suites (SW-71, #576).

  • An external Rosette oracle drives Eshkol as a P7 differential lane. scripts/run_rosette_oracle.sh clones Rosette Wire at a pinned commit and drives its front-door Eshkol adapter, emitting one runtime event per backend comparison with a rendered receipt. Wired as an advisory, continue-on-error, non-required lane and as a high-severity criterion on the v1.3.5-evolve oracle target.

  • An infrastructure-disclosure gate scans PR text and every commit message in the incoming range. A squash merge had carried internal hostnames into permanent public history, because GitHub assembles a squash body from branch commit messages and review never treats that as a reviewable surface. scripts/check_disclosure.py runs two independent layers over commits, PR title and body, and added diff lines; the retained classes are private IPv4 ranges including CGNAT, SSH key-file references, ProxyCommand, tailscale nc and MAC addresses. CI requires an actual incoming-range scan for pass evidence.

  • Release readiness is bound to the exact checkout, and pending evidence blocks the release. scripts/release_readiness_guard.py ships with its own test suite and is wired into the release workflow; ICC_BIN no longer defaults to a maintainer home-directory path, and an empty or non-executable value fails loud. The GPU gate’s tolerance override is deleted: the workflow pinned a tolerance five orders of magnitude looser than the script’s own default, so the first self-hosted GPU runner would have certified correctness against it.

  • Doc-claim allowlisting is line-independent. A claim id hashed the line number, so a dated changelog entry re-hashed every release simply because content was prepended above it; the key now excludes the line, and “stale” requires both keys dead. Generated API docs are gated by gen_api_docs.py --check in the assurance job and by a high-severity oracle criterion, after four pull requests added public headers without regenerating — 16 stale or missing pages, six of them headers with no page at all.

  • Parity and gap evidence are canonical. All 330 VM gap rows carry an explicit disposition with live reproducers, generated JIT and VM arity probes agree on valid calls and give matching fatal diagnostics on invalid ones, and overall and high-risk differential coverage floors are wired into the oracle (PR-03, PR-05, PR-10, SW-101, SW-102). Two divergences are deliberately left unbaselined and tracked, and the axis-3 baseline records a pre-existing three-contract split on string-contains, measured on a build carrying none of this release’s commits.

  • A linux-x64-debug required context builds Debug with assertions and switch warnings and runs focused CTest plus the exhaustive-dispatch gate on every pull request, with all NDEBUG blocks audited and compiled in both configurations (#573). The ASan/UBSan lane sets explicit sanitizer environments per step and the leak gate uses the GCC-matched driver (#584). Required workflows run on merge_group events with the docs-only file set computed from the merge-group base and head (#577).

  • Self-hosted mesh runners are ephemeral and hardened: a pinned image running non-root, a launcher minting one-shot registration tokens from a host-only file with log redaction, removable containers with a read-only root, isolated work and read-only dependency mounts with resource limits, workflow permissions defaulting to read-only contents with no id-token and per-job timeouts, and fork pull requests that never reach the mesh (#574). A shared idempotent FetchContent source cache lets a fresh configure succeed fully disconnected (#542).

  • Eshkol ships its own discovery contract. cmake/FindEshkol.cmake resolves the compiler, library, stdlib.o and stdlib module directory against the real installed layout, and the Eshkol::eshkol imported target carries the runtime archive, stdlib.o and the platform frameworks as one complete interface link set. This closes two undocumented traps: the plausible guess eshkol-static resolves to the compiler aggregate rather than the lean runtime, and every compiled program calls __eshkol_lib_init__, defined only inside stdlib.o. cmake --install ships the Find module so find_package(Eshkol) works from a source install (#496, #515).

  • Substrate: the ADR-0000 Stage 1 and Stage 2 gates close. An LLVM-free workspace resolver, the user-facing eshkol check and eshkol doc modules commands, and the shared resolver integrated into the VM, LSP, REPL and tooling; generated import aliases replaced by binding-metadata rewrites. Alongside them: scalar and tensor AD counters with a strict-mode query, NodeId-keyed BindingId, NominalTypeId, TypeRef and typed metadata, a recursive import algebra, Diagnostic v1, and LLVM post-macro resolution.

  • The bench PGO corpus is a Stage-1 smoke consumer instead of a zero-caller asset that had already bit-rotted: every corpus program runs under JIT and AOT and must exit 0, produce non-empty output, and produce byte-identical output between the two modes, with an empty or missing corpus graded NO_DATA rather than passing vacuously (ADR-0007, #490).
